# V11.0 UX Map — packages UX-1 (audit), UX-2 (Find Anything), UX-3 (Settings layers), UX-4 (What's in the game), UX-5 (Docked panel) and UX-6 (Switch-over)

**UX-1** audited `(V10.1) australia-game-with-ai.tsx` as shipped (282,211 lines, md5 `a7d56ae0e89666564909bacaaa7370df`): every number in the *today* columns was read from the file's own registries or measured by driving the real game in Chromium; nothing is estimated unless it says *derived*. **UX-2** (Find Anything) then changed the file; what it changed is in §16 and marked in the tables (*UX-2* in *Moves in*, the 28 new ids in §4, the new section and setting in §6 and §7). The *today* columns still describe V10.1 with Find Anything off, which is exactly how V11.0 behaves with the classic layout on (UX-6). This map is the input for UX-3 to UX-6 and for the test that walks it (`v110fa` walks every id below).

## Status after UX-6

- **The new layout is on by default.** The four V11.0 switches (`findAnythingEnabled`, `settingsLayersEnabled`, `whatsInTheGameEnabled`, `dockedPanelEnabled`) default to on: a new player starts with 🔎 Find Anything, ⭐ Essentials and the five goal groups, 🧩 What's in the game and the 📑 side panel. No other default changed (V10.1's 599 defaults keep their fingerprint).
- **A returning player is moved once.** A browser with a first-run record or stored preferences gets the four switches set on, once, and a one-time notice on the main menu that names the classic switch; a small record (`aussie.v110.layoutSwitchOver`) remembers the move and the notice, so after that the player's own choices stick (an "off" is stored now that it differs from the default).
- **One classic switch**, `classicLayoutV101` (Classic layout (V10.1); profile, off by default, never in a save). While it is on, the four predicates read false and the game looks and works as V10.1 did; the four switches' stored values are kept, so turning it off brings back what the player had. It is on the main menu (in both layouts), in ⭐ Essentials, in Find Anything ("classic", "old layout", "v10.1") and in the classic view's Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 🕰️ Classic layout.
- **One look for the new screens** (SECTION 20BT): shared spacing, type sizes (rem or the 11 px the Text size setting scales), button shapes and a keyboard-only focus ring for Find Anything, Essentials and the group bar, What's in the game and the side panel. The classic layout is untouched.
- **Clean-ups carried from UX-3 to UX-5:** Negotiations lists each option once in the layered view; the floating widgets, the classic quick bar and the turn pill make room for the side panel; "dock" finds the side panel; a setting opened from Find Anything lands on its row; the side panel's two effects list complete dependencies (no lint suppression); with the classic layout off the twins read the label table (Travel Discipline Strength; Floating UI Conflict Control Options).
- **Self-tests:** 3,216 checks in 83 suites, all pass (3,176 checks in 82 suites before; +40 in `v116so`; 28 older checks updated, none removed), release check 158 of 158 (READY_WITH_WARNINGS, the same warnings: K01–K03), Co-Pilot 85 of 85, strict TypeScript 0 errors. The rules stamp stays 1010, and real V9.7, V9.8, V10.0 and V10.1 saves play the same three days as the V10.1 build itself, with the classic layout on and off.

## Status after UX-5

- **Docked side panel** (off by default, `dockedPanelEnabled`, profile setting, in Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 📑 Side panel). Off, every window opens exactly as UX-4 delivered it (checked in Chromium: in the same seeded match the six windows draw byte-identical DOM and behave the same). On, in a match, on a screen at least 1024 px wide:
  - **one side panel beside the map** with an id of its own (`docked_panel`: a side panel the surface manager tracks and never counts as blocking) and five tabs: 💰 Market (💰 Sell and 🛍️ Buy inside), 🎯 Challenges, ✈️ Travel, 🔨 Workshop, 🏦 Investments. The page is padded so the panel sits beside the map instead of over it; the header, the map and End Turn stay usable.
  - **each tab draws its window's own content with its own handlers**: the window's element is built exactly as before and only drawn in the panel instead of over the page (`v115Dock.slot`); nothing is forked.
  - **every way in opens the matching tab**: the buttons, C / T / R / M / W (R and M the two halves of Market), Find Anything, What's in the game's cards, 🧠 INTELLIGENCE, Ask answers and Co-Pilot "Open …" buttons, the guided first match's buttons and the windows' own links. One tab at a time; the tab and Market's half are remembered for the session (interface state: a save never carries it).
  - **not a dialog**: a labelled region with a tab list (← → Home End), focus in on open and back to the opener on close, no focus trap. C T R M W, G and Space work while it is open; Escape and the rescue close it; every window and decision (the 12 of V10.1 and the 5 UX-2 registered) opens over it; the End Turn chip and the toasts on the right move to its left.
  - **narrower than 1024 px**, or outside a match, the six open as windows, as before.
- **The release check reports a row that could not run as skipped** (never a pass, never a failure): the panel's rendered-markup check needs the test runners' static renderer, which the in-game LAB does not have.
- **Self-tests:** 3,176 checks in 82 suites pass (3,136 in 81 before; +40 in `v115dp`), release check 157 of 157, Co-Pilot 85 of 85, strict TypeScript 0 errors. The rules stamp stays 1010, and old saves play move for move with all four V11.0 switches on.

## Status after UX-4

- **What's in the game** (off by default, `whatsInTheGameEnabled`, profile setting, in Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 🧩 What's in the game). Off, the game is exactly what UX-3 delivered plus this one row. On, a window with an id of its own (`whats_in_the_game`) shows:
  - **101 cards over 109 switch keys**, one for each feature §7 marks *card:*, under the five goal groups (🎮 Match & rules 42 · 🤖 Your rival 11 · 👥 Teams & automation 27 · 🧭 Help, screen & sound 11 · 🔬 Records & tools 10), with an All / On / Off filter and a count of how many are on. The 13 national cards are built from `EXPERIENCE_SYSTEM_REGISTRY`.
  - **Each card** names the feature from the shared label table (a card with one switch by that switch; the 8 cards with two switches by a feature name in the same table) and says in one plain sentence what it does and whether it is on. It shows its real switch, which writes through the very call its control in Settings makes; the 3 switches no control changes are shown read-only. It lists what the feature needs (a parent switch, an experience mode, Team Mode, a match), each marked met or not, links to the feature's home in Settings and, while the feature is on, opens its window (35 cards have one).
  - **Team cards follow the Teams gate** of the layered Settings: until a Team Mode match runs or Competitive AI is on, 24 team cards wait (the page says so), while Competitive AI, Auto Mode and Human automation show. The page has no *Show inactive*: none of its switches is a setting with no effect.
  - **Ways in:** 🧩 on the main menu, in ⋯ More and the full action bar, on the Settings group bar (layers on), on the switch's own row, and in Find Anything. There every card is a result, and "features" or "what's in the game" open the page.
- **Labels:** "Travel Discipline" and "Floating UI Conflict Control" each named two settings; the companions are now *Travel Discipline Strength* and *Floating UI Conflict Control Options*. The two settings still titled with their key in words are now *Auto Mode Master Switch* and *Directive Budget Limit*. These are label-table changes: the classic Settings rows keep their titles.
- **The Balance Lab's three settings** are homed in 🔬 Records & tools › ⚖️ Balance Lab, where their controls are, instead of 🤖 Your rival › Strategy Lab Presets, which has none (Your rival 104 → 101, Records & tools 62 → 65).
- **Self-tests:** 3,136 checks in 81 suites pass (3,096 in 80 before; +40 in `v114wg`), release check 156 of 156, Co-Pilot 85 of 85, strict TypeScript 0 errors. The rules stamp stays 1010, and old saves play move for move with all three V11.0 switches on.

## Status after UX-3

- **Settings layers** (off by default, `settingsLayersEnabled`, profile setting, in Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 🗂️ Settings Layout). Off, Settings is exactly what UX-2 delivered plus this one row (checked in the browser: every tab draws the same sections, controls and words, apart from the counts that now include the new switch). On:
  - **⭐ Essentials** takes the Simple view's place: the 12 choices of §6, each writing through the call the game already uses (the Simple view's handler, `SET_AI_DIFFICULTY` for the rival's difficulty, the Learn tab's mode change, the theme switch, the Text size row). A rule a running V10.1 match locks says so and that the change waits for the next match.
  - **Five goal groups** replace the 11 tabs (🎮 Match & rules 100 · 🤖 Your rival 104 · 👥 Teams & automation 251 · 🧭 Help, screen & sound 63 · 🔬 Records & tools 62; Essentials 15; 6 internal). Every section is drawn as it was; the settings search still searches everything and shows each hit in its home.
  - **One home per setting** (the V11.0 home column of §7): 7 sections are dissolved into their settings' homes, 5 blocks of controls are drawn in their home instead of where they were, 29 rows give a home to settings whose control lived in a window, the LAB or a version section (each writes through that control's own call), and 11 duplicate or misplaced controls are not drawn away from home.
  - **Teams & automation** shows the Team Mode AI master switch and the three solo sections (Auto Mode, Human Player Automation, Priority Resolution — 24 settings) until a Team Mode match runs or `teamCompetitiveAiEnabled` is on; then all 27 sections and the Team Intelligence OS rows.
  - **Show inactive** reveals the 20 settings with no effect in this version and `deterministicRngActive` (8 have a control in a section; the other 13 have none in Settings and appear in the read-only list); **🔒 Set by presets** lists, read-only with their value, the 55 settings no control in Settings changes: 44 with no control, `deterministicRngActive`, 5 only a reset changes, and 5 with no effect that nothing draws (two Adaptive AI values, three replay switches only Replay Studio's presets set).
- **Find Anything fixes:** matching only at the start of words (a squashed key only from its start: "tent" and "rain" now find no setting; UX-2 returned 15 each), the everyday words of the brief, one label table (329 settings whose title was their key in words now have written labels; never "Ai"), the 12 Essentials as results, results that open the new homes while the layers are on, and Ctrl/⌘+K in the Help window while Find Anything is on.
- **Self-tests:** 3,096 checks in 80 suites pass (3,056 in 79 before; +40 in `v113sl`), release check 155 of 155, Co-Pilot 85 of 85, strict TypeScript 0 errors; the rules stamp stays 1010 and old saves play move for move with both switches on.

## Status after UX-2

- **Find Anything** (off by default, `findAnythingEnabled`, profile setting): one search box over every surface and window in §4 and §5 (the 8 unused ids aside), 88 settings sections, all 600 settings (by name or key, including the 29 the settings search misses), 17 shortcut groups, 48 LAB cards and Ask. Ctrl/⌘+K and a 🔎 Find button open it; results open through the buttons' own calls (§16).
- **Registry (Q4):** the 61 V10.1 ids are kept; 28 ids added (27 windows from §5, and Find Anything); the 8 ids nothing opens are marked `not used in V10.1`; the shared ids each keep one window while Find Anything is on (§12.1).
- **Version:** GAME_VERSION 11.0.0, a new save-ladder rung that changes nothing, rules stamp still 1010; matches, saves and replays play move for move.
- **Self-tests:** 3,056 checks in 79 suites pass (3,015 in 78 before; +41 in `v110fa`), release check 154 of 154, Co-Pilot 85 of 85, strict TypeScript 0 errors. The LAB now shows skipped checks as skipped (Q8).

## At a glance (V10.1, the UX-1 audit)

- **Surfaces.** Of the 61 registry surfaces, 16 open in one click from the play screen and 9 more in two (measured); the rest are always on screen (the play view, HUD, quick bar, toasts), appear only after a switch or an event, or never open: **8 ids have no opener at all** (§11.1) and **5 ids are shared by two different windows** (§12.1). **28 real windows and panels have no id** (§5), so the surface manager, Escape and the rescue cannot see them.
- **Settings navigation.** *More › ⚙️ Settings* opens the **Simple** view (22 choices). The hub is one more click; its tabs are 2 clicks away through a ⋯ More shortcut or a LAB card, except **Economy & Money and Competition & Regions, which take 4**.
- **Settings you cannot find.** The game's own settings search (`searchSettingsHub`, run for every key) finds 570 of 599; the **29 it cannot find** are the keys missing from the hub index — 12 of the 13 national-system switches and 7 replay switches that are on screen in other sections, the Team OS switches in LAB and the Guided Learning choices in INTELLIGENCE (§11.5).
- **Settings you cannot change.** 45 indexed settings have **no control anywhere** and 5 only a reset touches (§11.3); 18 have their control somewhere other than where search sends you — the Guardian AI Center window, the Activity Ledger window or another section (§11.4).
- **Duplicates.** 15 settings are indexed in more than one section; the 22 Simple-view settings each have a second control in the hub; ⋯ More and *View All Actions* open the same popover; *More › 🎬 Replay* opens replay **settings**, not the Replay Studio (§12).
- **Shortcuts.** 17 shortcut groups; **Ctrl+K is already taken** by the optional Ask the Game AI drawer (Q5); an Alt+S Strategy Planner hotkey exists but is never wired.
- **The brief's numbers** all match the file except the self-test baseline (Q1). §13 lists the eight questions and the decisions the UX-2 brief gave.

## 1. How to read this map

**Starting point for every click count:** a fresh browser, the guided first match skipped with *Skip tutorial — open the full game* (not counted), V10.1 defaults, an AI Opponent match on the **PLAY** layer, 1280×800. A click is one press; scrolling and typing are not clicks. Keyboard alternatives are listed separately.

- **measured** — the path was replayed from a fresh page in Chromium and the game's own surface manager (`window.__GLOBAL_UI_SURFACE_MANAGER__`) or UI state confirmed the result.
- **derived** — computed from the code (the opener's conditions) plus a measured base path, because the default state hides it (e.g. a feature that is off).
- **Moves in** names the package that changes the item's home. Every row also gets a Find Anything entry in **UX-2**; that is not repeated per row. **UX-6** turned the new layout on by default, restyled the four new V11.0 screens (the brief: new screens only, the classic layout untouched) and added the classic switch that restores the V10.1 home shown in the *today* column; a *UX-6* in *Moves in* on a V10.1 window means the classic switch brings its V10.1 look back, not a restyle.
- **V11.0 group** is the UX-3 home (built: Settings shows it once `settingsLayersEnabled` is on, §17): ⭐ Essentials · 🎮 Match & rules · 🤖 Your rival · 👥 Teams & automation · 🧭 Help, screen & sound · 🔬 Records & tools.
- **ids are the contract.** The UX-2+ walk test should iterate the ids in sections 4–10 and assert each is still reachable; ids never change in this round.

## 3. How the game is navigated today

The containers everything else hangs off. Clicks are from the starting point in §1.

| Container | How to open | Clicks | What is inside |
|---|---|---|---|
| PLAY layer (default) | already open | 0 | Header (layers, Co-Pilot control bar), HUD (day, actions, cash, win race, End Turn), quick bar (Challenges, Explore, Resource Market, Emergency Loan, ⋯ tools, ✕), play card (Current Focus, Situation, Recommended Action, other options, *View All Actions*), system breakdown, match standing, **action bar** (Save / Load, Challenges, Travel, Market, End Turn, Workshop, Resource Market, Contracts, Progress, character ability, **⋯ More**), region panel, map with region preview and the **map footer** buttons (Scenarios, Career, Diplomacy, Migration, Determinism + optional feature buttons) |
| ⋯ More (action bar) | click *⋯ More* | 1 | MORE · BUILD & COMPETE: Negotiations, Governance, 🧠 (AI & team settings — icon only), Automation, Replay, Diagnostics, ⚙️ Settings, ❓ Help, 📜 History, 🚪 Leave match (the last three are below the fold at 1280×800) |
| INTELLIGENCE layer | click *🧠 INTELLIGENCE* (or any *Why?*, *What If?*, *View Plan*, *Change Goal*, the win-race chip) | 1 | 10 sub-tabs (Ask, Learn, Situations, Strategy, Situation, World, Team, Rivals, Diplomacy, What-If), Ask chips, Guided Learning mode, Learn centre, Background AI workspace, contextual actions, Co-Pilot control modes and explain options, *Open Activity Ledger* |
| LAB layer | click *🧪 LAB* | 1 | 48 cards in 8 groups: 33 open Settings (30 on an exact section, 3 on a tab), 15 open a window (3 disabled until their switch is on); then the technical Co-Pilot state, *Run self-tests* and 17 inspectors |
| Settings (Simple view) | More › ⚙️ Settings | 2 | 22 choices in 5 groups (Match, Gameplay, Rival, Look & sound, Help); *Show all* → Advanced |
| Settings (Advanced = the hub) | More › Settings › ⚙️ Advanced Settings | 3 | 11 tabs, 86 indexed sections, search (`/` or Ctrl+F), basic/advanced view switch, presets, filters |
| Settings (Game Experience view) | More › Settings › 🎮 Game Experience | 3 | Experience profile / plan screen |
| Main menu | ⋯ More › 🚪 Leave match › Leave without saving (or Save & leave, which downloads the match first) | 3 | Theme (Dark/Light/Retro/System), character, name, experience mode, 6 game modes, Save / Load, Settings, "Press ? for how to play" |
| Floating widgets | off by default — turned on in Settings | — | Guardian button, AI Command Center widget, Ask button/drawer, Unified Launcher, Widget Dock |
| Keyboard | — | 0 | see §10 |

## 4. Surfaces: the registry (61 V10.1 ids + 28 added in UX-2 + 1 in UX-4 + 1 in UX-5)

Rows 1–61 are V10.1's ids (kept, Q4); rows 62–89 are the ids UX-2 added (Q4): 27 windows that had none (§5) and Find Anything; row 90 is UX-4's What's in the game page; row 91 is UX-5's side panel. A new id's *Flags* are the ones the V11.0 table maps to it while Find Anything is on ("registered, not tracked" = its state is not an interface flag, so it has an id for Find Anything and the walk test but the surface manager does not track it). Ids marked *not used in V10.1* open nothing (§11.1).

*Kind*: panel / widget / toast / launcher / drawer / modal / overlay as registered. *Opens today*: the shortest measured path (**m**) or the code path (**d**). *Flags*: the `uiState` flags the surface manager maps to the id. *Plan*: dock = a tab of the UX-5 side panel; dock? = proposed extra tab (Q3); decision = stays a blocking modal; window = stays a window (Q3); panel = already non-modal.

## 5. Windows and panels the registry did not know (ids since UX-2)

Real, reachable UI that V10.1 drew without a `UI_SURFACE_REGISTRY` id (the surface manager could not see it, so Escape, the rescue and shortcut suppression treated it as absent unless a fallback existed). **UX-2 gave each an id (Q4)**: the id is in the first column, and *Id now* shows where it is registered (§4). The Ask drawer is the live window behind the shared id `ask_game_ai`, so it keeps that id.

## 6. Settings sections

91 indexed sections plus 1 rendered section the index does not list (Balance Lab). *Clicks* = the shortest measured route from the play screen: a ⋯ More shortcut or a LAB card opens Settings directly on a tab (2 clicks), otherwise More › Settings (opens on the Simple view) › ⚙️ Advanced Settings › tab (4; Settings Home 3). Scrolling within a tab is not a click; sections start expanded (`startSettingsCategoryCollapsed` is off).

**Proposed groups (UX-3).** **🎮 Match & rules** — Set up how a match plays: length, goal, actions, challenges, money, regions and content. (20 sections) · **🤖 Your rival** — How strong your rival is, how it thinks and what it remembers. (10 sections) · **👥 Teams & automation** — Team Mode, AI teammates, automation and oversight. (27 sections) · **🧭 Help, screen & sound** — Guidance, assistants, look, sound, accessibility and layout. (23 sections) · **🔬 Records & tools** — Replays, records, diagnostics, balance tools and the reference library. (10 sections) Built in UX-3 (§17): the dissolved sections draw their settings in their homes; the Balance Lab is under Records & tools.

**Essentials page (12 choices).**

| # | Choice | Keys | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Experience | `experienceMode` | Explorer / Nation / Full decides how much of the game is on screen. |
| 2 | How to win | `winCondition` | The victory goal; locked while a match runs (V10.1). |
| 3 | Game length | `totalDays` | Days on the calendar; locked while a match runs (V10.1). |
| 4 | Actions per day | `actionLimitsEnabled`, `playerActionsPerDay` | The daily action budget and its on/off switch, as one choice. |
| 5 | Rival difficulty | `(match state) gameState.aiDifficulty` | Q6: writes match state through SET_AI_DIFFICULTY, the path Quick Setup and the menu picker use (locked while a match runs). The settings key `aiDifficulty` is never written and stays internal. |
| 6 | Keep my match safe | `matchRecoveryEnabled`, `warnBeforeUnloadEnabled` | Autosave & Resume and the leave-site warning: the V10.1 diagnosis ranked silent progress loss #3. |
| 7 | How much help | `guidedLearning`, `guidedFirstRunEnabled`, `playerIntentOnboardingEnabled` | Q6: Guided Learning mode (Off … Teaching), the guided first match and the onboarding hints, as one choice. |
| 8 | Rival turn speed | `rivalTurnPace` | Q6: how quickly the rival's turn is shown; takes the slot freed by folding Guided first match into How much help. |
| 9 | Look | `visualMode`, `(profile) theme` | Enhanced/classic look and the Dark/Light/Retro/System theme. |
| 10 | Text size | `accessibilitySettings.textScaleMultiplier` | A−/A+ (V10.1). |
| 11 | Motion | `reducedMotion` | Follow system / reduce / full motion. |
| 12 | Sound | `soundEnabled`, `soundVolume` | Sound effects and volume, as one choice. |

## 7. Every setting (604 keys)

Ordered by today's tab and section; keys not in the index come last. *Scope*: **match** = a save carries it; **profile** = the player's, kept on this device (`V99_PERSISTED_PROFILE_SETTING_KEYS`), never taken from a save; *profile (not stored)* = profile-scoped but not remembered between visits. *Clicks*: to reach the control from the play screen by the shortest measured route (Simple view 2; a ⋯ More shortcut or LAB card 2; More › Settings › Advanced › tab 4), +1 where the code shows the control only in the hub's Advanced view. "Shown when X on" means the control appears only after another switch is on (from the code's own conditions, confirmed by the probe where it ran). *Notes*: inactive = flagged "No effect in this version" (goes behind UX-3's *show inactive* toggle). *card:* names the feature's card on UX-4's What's in the game page as the page names it (the UX-1 working name follows where it differs).

## 11. Unreachable today

### 11.1 Registry surfaces nothing in game code opens

Each was checked three ways: no button in the crawl reached it, no game-code line sets any of its flags (self-tests excluded), and no `openSurface()` call names it.

| id | What happens instead | Proposal (Q4) | UX-2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| `guardian_side_panel` | The Guardian button's "side panel" style opens `guardian_status_panel`; `showGuardianSidePanel` is never set. | Keep the id; point it at the Status Panel (alias) so nothing that names it breaks. | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |
| `developer_diagnostics_drawer` | The real Diagnostics Drawer (Ctrl+Alt+D, LAB card, More › Diagnostics) uses `showAutoModeDiagnosticsDrawer`, which the registry does not map. | Map `showAutoModeDiagnosticsDrawer` to this id. | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |
| `guardian_warning_modal` | Guardian warnings render inline (`GuardianInlineWarning` beside End Turn, travel, sell, contracts, infrastructure, expeditions). Only a self-test sets the flag. | Keep the id (blocking warnings may come back); mark it "not used" in the walk test. | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |
| `treasury_requests_dialog` | Treasury requests are handled in the Team Treasury settings and Overseer dashboard; none of the three flags is set. | Keep the id; mark "not used". | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |
| `governor_exception_dialog` | Governor exceptions are recorded in team state (`team.governorExceptions`) with no dialog; none of the three flags is set. | Keep the id; mark "not used". | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |
| `double_or_nothing_modal` | Double or Nothing is a notification plus an inline choice after a challenge win. | Keep the id; mark "not used". | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |
| `rescue_emergency_overlay` | Shift+Esc runs the UI rescue directly; no overlay opens. | Keep the id; mark "not used". | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |
| `stats_modal` | `showStats` is set by an Ask-the-Game-AI deep link (tab "player"), but nothing renders it: the flag opens nothing. | Wire the deep link to the Progress dashboard, or give Stats a renderer. | kept, marked `not used in V10.1`; never a Find Anything result |

### 11.2 UI with a renderer but no opener

- **Auto Mode activity panel** — rendered when `uiState.showAutoModeActivityPanel` is true (line 281492), but no game code sets it; the registry lists the flag as an alias of `activity_ledger`.
- **Alt+S Strategy Planner hotkey** — `useStrategyPlannerHotkey` (line 21941) is defined and never called.
- **UX-2:** the Auto Mode activity panel has its own id (`auto_mode_activity_panel`) and a Find Anything result that opens it when Auto Mode is on; Alt+S is listed in Find Anything, whose result opens the planner the way the Launcher does (the key itself is still not wired).

### 11.5 Not searchable today

- **Measured:** the game's own `searchSettingsHub` was run for every key (by its name, its name split into words, and its label). It finds **570 of 599** — 120 as their own result, 450 only through their section. It **cannot find 29**: `opponentGenomeId`, `replaySmartCheckpointsEnabled`, `replayTurningPointDetectionEnabled`, `replayDeterministicValidationEnabled`, `replayWhatIfBranchingEnabled`, `replayDeveloperDiagnosticsEnabled`, `replayAutoOpenAtMatchEnd`, `replayDetailLevel`, `playerIntentOnboardingEnabled`, `playerIntentOnboardingDismissed`, `guidedLearning`, `industriesEnabled`, `infraNetworksEnabled`, `megaprojectsEnabled`, `nationalDevelopmentEnabled`, `innovationEnabled`, `systemicRiskEnabled`, `nationalPrioritiesEnabled`, `economicGeographyEnabled`, `workforceHumanCapitalEnabled`, `stateCapacityEnabled`, `publicFinanceEnabled`, `householdsEnabled`, `playerIntentExplainRoutineActions`, `playerIntentExplainMode`, `teamIntelligenceOsEnabled`, `teamOsAuthorityLevel`, `teamOsEnemyEnabled`, `teamOsFullInspectionEnabled`.
- **30 settings are not in `SETTINGS_HUB_SECTION_INDEX`** (the 29 above plus `nationalSystemsEnabled`, which Match Variety's keywords happen to match). Their real homes are in §7: the 13 national-system switches are rendered in Match Variety, 7 replay switches in AI Difficulty.
- **The Balance Lab section** (`advancedSystems.balanceLab`, Replay & Diagnostics) is rendered but not indexed.
- **UX-2:** Find Anything finds every one of the 600 settings by its key (first result) and by each of its labels, the 29 above included (key first, label in the top 10; `v110fa_19` to `v110fa_21`), and the Balance Lab section. Each opens where its control really is (§11.4 routes are used), and a setting with no control says so in its result (§11.3).
- **Settings that live only outside Settings:** the four Team OS keys (LAB › Team Intelligence 2.0 inspector), `guidedLearning` and the three `playerIntent*` choices (INTELLIGENCE), the Guardian button keys (Guardian AI Center window), the ledger housekeeping keys (Activity Ledger window).

### 11.6 Hidden until something else is switched on

- **Settings controls:** 46 keys got a working control only after the 51 switches that gate settings controls were turned on (probe pass *advanced, gates on*); the code's own conditions mark more ("shown when … on" in §7). They are reachable, one or more switches later.
- **Play-screen entry points off by default:** the floating Guardian button, AI Command Center widget and modal, Ask drawer, Unified Launcher, Widget Dock (the only route to the Strategy Planner), and the map-footer buttons for contracts, infrastructure, expeditions, crises, fog of war, What-If and campaign.
- **Disabled LAB cards until a switch is on:** AI Tuning Lab, AI Command Center, AI Action Pipeline Inspector.
- **Team Mode only:** the Overseer, Auditor and Pipeline buttons on the map footer need a Team Mode match plus 2–3 team switches (the LAB cards open the dashboards in any match).

### 12.6 Confusing twins

- **More › 🎬 Replay** opens the replay *settings* (Replay & Diagnostics tab); the Replay Studio itself opens from Save / Load, What-If, the result screen or a replay file.
- **More › 🔬 Diagnostics** opens the same settings tab *and* the Diagnostics Drawer.
- **Market** (sell, R) and **Resource Market** (buy, M) are two windows with near-identical names. *UX-5: with the side panel on they are the two halves of one 💰 Market tab (💰 Sell, 🛍️ Buy).*
- **⋯ More** and **View All Actions** open the same popover.
- Three different "Settings" entry points open three different views: More › Settings (Simple), LAB › Full Settings Hub (Simple), the More shortcuts and LAB cards (Advanced, a tab).
- **Travel Discipline** and **Floating UI Conflict Control** each named a switch and its companion (the strength; the options object). *UX-4: the companions are Travel Discipline Strength and Floating UI Conflict Control Options in the shared label table, which the layered rows, the read-only list, Find Anything and the cards use; the classic Settings rows keep their titles.* *UX-6 (the user's decision: the twins only): with the classic layout off the drawn rows read the label table too — the Team Brain slider says Travel Discipline Strength and the Floating UI row's change label says Floating UI Conflict Control Options; with it on, V10.1's titles.*

## 13. Where the file and the brief disagree — questions

| # | Topic | Detail (UX-1) | Decision (UX-2 brief) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Self-test baseline | The brief says 2,957 checks across 80 suites. This file gives 2,842 checks in 73 registered suites; 3,015 in 78 with the harness extras; 2,928 from the in-game LAB button; plus release check 153/153 and Co-Pilot 85/85 — all passing. Which runner should "none may drop" be measured with? (Proposal: node-run `--extras` = 3,015/78, plus release 153 and Co-Pilot 85, all recorded in each package report.) | Baseline = 3,015 checks in 78 suites (registered + `--extras`), release check 153 of 153, Co-Pilot 85 of 85, strict TypeScript 0 errors. After UX-2: 3,056 / 79, 154 / 154, 85 / 85, 0. |
| Q2 | One "Market" tab | UX-5 names five tabs; the game has two market windows (Market = sell, Resource Market = buy). Proposal: one Market tab with Sell and Buy inside. | One Market tab with Sell and Buy inside; R and M open the right half (UX-5). Done in UX-5 (§19). |
| Q3 | Modals that are not decisions | "Modals remain only for decisions that must block", but UX-5 names five tabs. 30 other registry windows are modal today without being decisions (table §4: 6 action windows marked dock?, 24 tool windows marked window). Proposal: UX-5 docks the five, adds the six action windows (Shop, Sabotage, Contracts, Infrastructure, Expeditions, Negotiations) plus Loans as extra tabs, and makes the 24 tool windows non-blocking windows; or keep them modal and leave them to a later round. Your call changes UX-5's size by about 3×. | UX-5 docks the five named tabs only; the six action windows and Loans wait; the 24 tool windows stay as they are. Done in UX-5 (§19): the five tabs; the six action windows, Loans and the 24 tool windows unchanged. |
| Q4 | Ids that open nothing, windows without ids | 8 registry ids never open (§11.1), 5 ids are shared by two windows (§12.1) and 28 real windows have no id (§5). "Lose nothing" suggests keeping every id. Proposal: keep all 61, add ids for the unregistered windows in UX-2, and let the walk test mark the 8 as "not used in V10.1" instead of failing. | Keep all 61 ids; add ids for the unregistered windows; give the 5 shared ids one window each; mark the 8 dead ids "not used in V10.1". Done in UX-2 (§4, §5, §12.1). |
| Q5 | Ctrl+K | Ctrl/⌘+K already toggles the Ask the Game AI drawer when `askGameAiUiEnabled` is on (off by default). Proposal: Ctrl+K opens Find Anything; Ask becomes a Find Anything result (and keeps its floating button). Alternative: Find Anything uses Ctrl+Shift+F or "/" when focus is on the page. | Ctrl/⌘+K opens Find Anything when it is on; when off, Ctrl/⌘+K behaves as today. Ask becomes a Find Anything result. Done in UX-2. |
| Q6 | Essentials | Proposed 12 choices in §6 (it adds Autosave, the leave warning, Guided Learning, theme and text size, which today sit outside the Simple view, and moves 11 feature switches from the Simple view to "What's in the game"). Confirm or edit. | Essentials approved with three edits: Rival difficulty writes match state (SET_AI_DIFFICULTY); Guided first match and playerIntentOnboardingEnabled fold into "How much help"; the freed slot becomes Rival turn speed (rivalTurnPace). (UX-3) |
| Q7 | Version-named sections | Settings has "✨ New in V9.9 / V10.0 / V10.1" sections. Proposal: UX-3 files each switch under its goal group (Autosave → Essentials, Rival Recap → Your rival, …) and UX-4 gives each a card; the version labels move to the What's New page. | Each switch filed under its goal group; version labels move to What's New. UX-2's own switch follows it (Interface & Accessibility › 🔎 Find Anything; V11.0 is in What's New). |
| Q8 | Skipped checks | The existing LAB runner reports 4 SKIPPED rows as `passed: true` (detail "SKIPPED: …"). New UX suites will report skips as skipped as the brief requires; should UX-2 also change the existing LAB runner, or leave V10.1 behaviour alone? | The LAB runner reports skips as skipped. Done in UX-2: 14 LAB rows that passed silently or "SKIPPED-as-pass" now count as skipped. |

## 15. How this map was made

- **Registries** were read from the running module (the file bundled by `tools/selftest/node-eval.mjs`), not from text: `UI_SURFACE_REGISTRY`, `SETTINGS_HUB_SECTION_INDEX`, `SETTINGS_HUB_TAB_ORDER/LABELS`, `DEFAULT_GAME_SETTINGS`, `V99/V101_INERT_SETTINGS`, `V95_MATCH/PROFILE_SCOPED_SETTING_KEYS`, `SIMPLE_SETTINGS`, `EXPERIENCE_MODE_OPTIONS`, `V95_EXISTING_SUITES`.
- **Code scans** used the TypeScript parser on the whole file: every `<SettingsSection>` and the keys its controls write, every `updateUiState` call that opens something (with its button and conditions), every settings write in game code, and which keys each section's code mentions.
- **Browser runs** (Chromium, the real game via Vite, Tailwind 3.4.17 built locally because the CDN is unreachable from the sandbox): a breadth-first click crawl from the play screen (48 one-click and 358 two-click paths, each replayed from a fresh page, reading `window.__GLOBAL_UI_SURFACE_MANAGER__` and the game's UI state); a settings probe that flipped 1479 controls in the hub (advanced and basic views with defaults, and the advanced view with the 51 gating switches on) and recorded which key each one wrote, restoring the setting after each (212 distinct keys got a working control; a control that changed nothing is never taken as proof of a missing control — the "no control" verdicts in §11.3 rest on the code scans); a path check of all 11 tabs and every LAB card; 24 targeted scenarios (a switch turned on, then the path clicked). Clicks that change the match (Do It, End Turn, travel, buy/sell, loans, Leave) were never used as steps except Leave for the menu route.
- **Not covered:** Team Mode matches (Team surfaces are derived from code), event-only surfaces (story decisions, approvals, day summary), the Game Experience plan flow, and touch input.
- UX-1 did not modify the game file (md5 before and after: `a7d56ae0e89666564909bacaaa7370df`). UX-2 changed it (§16); the *today* columns were kept from the UX-1 audit of the original file, and the map was re-checked against the changed module (`check-map.mjs`: every registry id, section, setting and nested field is listed once).
- UX-4's rebuild reads `data/registries-ux4.json` (`extract.mjs` on the file after UX-4; it now also lists the 101 cards with the names the page shows). `check-map.mjs` checks §4's plan columns and every *card:* note in §7 against the running module, and `check-homes.mjs` checks the V11.0 home column.
- UX-5's rebuild reads `data/registries-ux5.json` (`extract.mjs` on the file after UX-5; it now also lists the side panel's tabs, windows and threshold, and the map's plan lists suite `v115dp` walks). `check-map.mjs` also checks §4's side panel row, the six windows' homes and the plan column against them.
- UX-6's rebuild reads `data/registries-ux6.json` (`extract.mjs` on the file after UX-6; it now also lists the switch-over: the four defaults, the classic switch, its section, home and label, and the Find Anything counts) and `data/browser/ux6-hardening.json` (the browser checks and timings, §20). `check-map.mjs` also checks the classic switch's §7 row, its §6 section and the four defaults.

## 16. UX-2: Find Anything — what changed

All new code is in SECTION 20BL (foundations, ids) and SECTION 20BM (Find Anything) between SECTION 20BK and SECTION 21; every in-place edit elsewhere is marked `// V11.0 (SECTION 20Bx): …` (most are one or two lines; the largest is the call in the main component that hands the game's own handlers to Find Anything). Everything new is off until UX-6 (one profile setting, `findAnythingEnabled`, read with `=== true`).

| Area | What changed | Off (default) |
|---|---|---|
| Version | GAME_VERSION 11.0.0 (label V11.0); `migrateSaveTo1100` rung (changes nothing); rules stamp stays 1010; V10.1's What's New keeps its label written out; V11.0 leads What's New. | n/a: always |
| Switch | `findAnythingEnabled` (boolean, false, profile, persisted between visits, never in a save) in Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 🔎 Find Anything (Q7); in the Co-Pilot catalog; found by the settings search. | off |
| Find Anything | Dialog (combobox over a listbox, focus in and back, Esc closes, ↑↓ PgUp PgDn, Enter opens) from 🔎 Find in the play header or on the main menu, or Ctrl/⌘+K anywhere. Index of 853 entries: 8 actions (End turn, Save, Leave match, Clear notifications, Rescue the screen, Map, Text size, the Ask drawer) and 92 screens and panels (covering 80 surface ids: every used V10.1 id and every window of §5), 17 shortcut groups, 48 LAB cards, 88 settings sections, 600 settings; plus Ask the game for any text (first when it reads like a question). A result opens through the button's own call (interface flags, `navigateToSettings` with the field, the LAB card's `onOpen`, the game's handlers, the Ask box); one that cannot open says why (no match yet, not your turn, a switch that is off: Enter then opens that switch, the guided first match). | no button, no key listener, nothing rendered |
| Ctrl/⌘+K (Q5) | Opens Find Anything; the V10.1 Ask-drawer key stands down while Find Anything is on. | exactly V10.1 |
| Registry (Q4) | 28 ids added (27 windows + `find_anything`); 8 ids marked `not used in V10.1`; with Find Anything on the manager's table gives Help, Progress, Live Control Center and the Auto Mode panel their own ids and tracks Loans, the Co-Pilot console and recommendations; those windows read their stacking from their own id. | V10.1's table, the very same array |
| LAB (Q8) | A row that did not run is shown as skipped (– and "N skipped"), never as a pass; the 10 rendered-markup checks that used to return true without a renderer now say SKIPPED; the async-suite skip row says skipped; source-scan skips are marked too. | n/a: always |
| Self-tests | Suite `v110fa` (41 checks): version and rung, old saves load and play move for move with the switch on and off, the switch's scope and home, the map walk (61 surfaces, 28 windows, 87 sections, 599 settings + 1, 17 shortcuts, 48 LAB cards), every setting found by key and by label, ranking, Ask, availability, the router, the dialog's semantics, keys, no clock and no random draws, Q8. | — |

**Deferred (not this package):** the docked panels (UX-5), the settings layers and Essentials (UX-3), feature cards (UX-4), turning Find Anything on by default and the classic switch (UX-6). Alt+S stays unwired (Find Anything has a result for it). The LAB list's duplicate React keys (97 console warnings in V10.1 and after) are untouched.

**Where each thing moved (with the layered Settings on).** Keys, defaults, scopes and saved values never change; switching the layers off puts every control back where §7's *today* column says.

**Where the brief, the map and the file disagreed (and what UX-3 did).**

- *The 45 settings with no control* (§11.3) include `aiDifficulty`, but the map's own home for it (§7) is *internal*: it is never written, not even by a preset, and the brief replaces it with match state. UX-3 keeps it internal; `deterministicRngActive`, which only presets set, is the 45th row of the read-only list (behind Show inactive).
- *The 227 titles that were only the key in words*: counted the way UX-2 builds titles there are 329; all 329 have written labels.
- *Group counts*: Help, screen & sound is 63 with the new switch, and Records & tools 62 with `deterministicRngActive` (the brief: 62 and 61).
- *The theme* lives in the profile record and also in Settings › Appearance (§12.4 corrected).

**Deferred (not this package):** feature cards (UX-4), docked panels (UX-5), turning the layers and Find Anything on by default and the classic switch (UX-6). In Negotiations & Cash Transfers the moved "more negotiation options" block repeats three of the section's own options (both write the same `negotiationOptions` fields); merging the two lists is left for UX-6's restyle. The 45 settings with no control still have none (by the brief). *UX-6: the two lists are merged in the layered view (each option once) and the layers and Find Anything are on by default (§20).*

## 18. UX-4: What's in the game — what changed

All new code is in SECTION 20BP (the page, its cards, its switch and its ways in) and SECTION 20BQ (the Balance Lab's home and suite `v114wg`), between SECTION 20BO and SECTION 21. Every in-place edit elsewhere is marked `// V11.0 (SECTION 20BP): …` or `(SECTION 20BQ)`: 49 marked lines covering the switch's registration, the window's id and table entry, the Find Anything kind and search, the entry buttons, the label table's first lookup, the Balance Lab's home, and the counts that older suites pin.

| Area | What changed | Off (default) |
|---|---|---|
| Switch | `whatsInTheGameEnabled` (boolean, false, profile, remembered between visits, never in a save) in Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 🧩 What's in the game, with an *Open* button once it is on; in the Co-Pilot catalog; found by the settings search. | off |
| The page | A window with its own id, `whats_in_the_game` (tier 6 like Settings, drawn one step above it and below decisions). The surface manager tracks `showWhatsInTheGame` only while the switch is on, and Escape closes the page. It is a labelled dialog: a heading, the All / On / Off filter (a radio group), the count of features on among the cards shown ("16 of 77 features are on" in a fresh solo match, where the Teams gate holds 24 back; "… of 101" once it opens), then the cards under the five goal groups in Settings' order. | — |
| Cards | 101 over 109 switch keys: §7's *card:* marks, in the map's order; the 13 national cards are built from `EXPERIENCE_SYSTEM_REGISTRY`. Each card shows its name (a one-switch card takes its switch's label; a two-switch card takes a feature name from the same table), one plain sentence, On / Off / *On · not active yet*, its switches, its needs, ⚙️ Settings (its home) and, while it is on, *Open …* its window (35 cards). | — |
| Switches | Each writes through its Settings control's own call: the section's tracked call with that section's title, the labelled `updateGameSetting` call, or the panel's handler. Team Brain moves to the mode its toggle moves to, Persistent AI memory runs its own toggle (consent and storage), and Auto Mode drives the master toggle Settings shows. Switches that Settings moves together move together (Crises with Crisis chains, Campaign Mode with Campaign chapters), and with the layers on, Autosave & Resume and the leave-site warning write through Essentials. The 3 switches no control changes are shown read-only with their value: the infrastructure master switch and Expedition Mode, which only presets set, and the Overseer dashboard, which only a reset sets. No default changes. | — |
| Needs | A parent switch (the map's "shown when … on"): the card's switch waits for it, greyed out, its tooltip and `aria-describedby` pointing at that need, which the card lists once. The experience: the national switches wait for Nation or Full, and the 12 sub-systems also for National Systems. In a match, a rival, Team Mode or a V10.1 match; on the main menu these are described, not checked. Bank loans open to you, and the Guardian switched on. Secret objectives & Final Stretch, Daily Challenge (fixed rules) and Classic rules (V10.0) say they apply when a match starts, and a rule a running match locks says the change waits for the next match. | — |
| Groups and the gate | The five goal groups (🎮 42 · 🤖 11 · 👥 27 · 🧭 11 · 🔬 10). Team cards follow the Teams gate of the layered Settings: until a Team Mode match runs or Competitive AI is on, 24 wait (the page says how many), while Competitive AI, Auto Mode and Human automation show. There is no *Show inactive* (the user's decision), because none of the 109 switches is a setting with no effect. | — |
| Ways in | 🧩 *What's in the game* on the main menu (after 🔎 Find), in ⋯ More (before Leave) and the full action bar (after Settings), on the Settings group bar (layers on), on the switch's own row (*Open*), and in Find Anything. | the switch's row only |
| Find Anything | A new result kind, *Feature* (ranked between the Essentials and the shortcuts). While the page is on, the index gains the page and its 101 cards (869 → 971; 869 = UX-3's 867 + the new switch and its section). A card result opens the page at that card, whatever the filter. "features" and "what's in the game" put the page first, and while it is off, its switch. A question-shaped "what's in the game?" no longer puts Ask first; Ask stays in the list, and other questions are unchanged. | the very same index; the two phrases find the switch |
| Labels | Two pairs of twins split, two key-titled settings named, the new switch labelled and 8 feature names added (below). Label table only. | the label table is read by Find Anything and the layered Settings, both off by default; the classic rows keep their titles |

It checks that the 101 cards cover the map, the national cards come from the registry, names come from the label table, and each card has one sentence, a home and a goal group. It reads every write path from the game's own source (108 of 109; Auto Mode has a check of its own) and covers flips, pairs, Essentials, Auto Mode, Team Brain, persistent memory and the read-only switches. It covers parent, experience and match needs, the next match and locks, no default changes, the groups, the filter and count, the Teams gate, no *Show inactive*, links to homes and windows, and the ways in. For Find Anything: results, phrases and card routes. Then the window id and the manager, the dialog's semantics, the map walk (every id of §4–§10 still there and reachable), the labels and the Balance Lab's home. Last: no clock and no random draw, rules stamp 1010, real V9.7, V9.8 and V10.0 saves move for move with all three V11.0 switches on, the status words and the switch's row. | — |

**The Balance Lab's home.** `balanceLabEnabled`, `balanceLabSandboxMode` and `balanceLabAutoStressTracking` were homed in 🤖 Your rival › Strategy Lab Presets (where the hub index files them), which draws no control for any of them. Their controls are the Activity Ledger window's Balance Lab tab and, for Auto-Stress Tracking, the Balance Lab panel in Replay & Diagnostics. UX-4 homes all three in 🔬 Records & tools › ⚖️ Balance Lab. With the layered Settings on, two rows there write through the window's own calls (`updateGameSetting` with its labels and section); Find Anything opens that section in the classic Settings too. Your rival goes from 104 to 101 settings and Records & tools from 62 to 65 (the new switch takes Help, screen & sound from 63 to 64).

**Where the brief, the map and the file disagreed (and what UX-4 did).**

- *Show inactive on the page*: none of the 109 card switches is one of the 20 settings with no effect, so a toggle would hide nothing. The page has none (the user's decision); the 20 stay behind *Show inactive* in the layered Settings and in Find Anything.
- *Card names*: the map's card names were UX-1 working names, not labels from the table. The page names a one-switch card by its switch's label and gives the 8 two-switch cards a feature name in the same table (the user's decision); 56 of the 101 differ, and §7's notes now carry the page's name.
- *The Balance Lab*: the map and UX-3 homed its three settings in a section with no control for them. UX-4 homes them where their controls are (the user's decision).
- *The twins*: label table only (the user's decision). Classic Settings still titles both rows "Travel Discipline" and both "Floating UI Conflict Control".
- *Auto Mode*: the map files the card under the top-level `autoModeEnabled`, but the toggle Settings shows and the Auto Mode panels read `autoModeSettings.autoModeEnabled` (with `.enabled`). The card drives and reads that toggle, as Settings does, and leaves the top-level key as it was (only the old Auto Mode boundaries code writes it).
- *"Next match"*: UX-1's working notes (`plan.cjs`) say 22 card features take effect at the next match. The code reads 20 of them as the settings stand: the 13 national systems through `isSystemVisible` every turn, the classic rival through `v100RivalPolicyActive`, and the rival's personality, the challenge board, the challenge approaches, the dynamic situations and the races where they are used. The secret objectives are dealt when a match starts, and Classic rules (V10.0) stamps a new match; those two cards, and Daily Challenge (fixed rules), say they apply when a match starts.
- *Corrected*: §7 said the settings search cannot find `settingsLayersEnabled`. That came from search data older than the key; the search finds it as a field. The builder now marks only keys the search run covered.

**Deferred (not this package):** docked panels (UX-5); turning the page, the layers and Find Anything on by default, and the classic switch (UX-6). The classic Settings rows keep the twin titles. In the classic Settings the Balance Lab section still draws only Auto-Stress Tracking; its other two switches stay in the Activity Ledger window, on the card and, with the layers on, as rows in the section. The top-level `autoModeEnabled` key keeps its row and its one writer. *UX-6: on by default, the classic switch added, and the twins' drawn rows read the label table with the classic layout off (§20).*

## 19. UX-5: Docked panel — what changed

All new code is in SECTION 20BR (the panel: its switch, its id, the tabs, the one-tab rule, the view and the hook) and SECTION 20BS (the map walk and suite `v115dp`), between SECTION 20BQ and SECTION 21. Every in-place edit elsewhere is marked `// V11.0 (SECTION 20BR): …` or `(SECTION 20BS)`: 55 marked places (105 lines, 38 of them changing a line that was there) covering the switch's registration, the panel's id and its row in the surface manager's table, the one line in `updateUiState`, the keys, Escape and the rescue, the six windows' slots, the page's padding and the panel itself, the switch's row, Find Anything's result and phrase, the release check's skipped rows, and the counts older suites pin.

| Area | What changed | Off (default) |
|---|---|---|
| Switch | `dockedPanelEnabled` (boolean, false, profile, remembered between visits, never in a save) in Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 📑 Side panel; in the Co-Pilot catalog; found by the settings search; homed in 🧭 Help, screen & sound with the other V11.0 switches. | off |
| The panel | A side panel with an id of its own, `docked_panel` (tier 5, the side-drawer zone, never blocking, no backdrop; Escape closes it), fixed at the right edge, `clamp(22rem, 32vw, 30rem)` wide, drawn at z 45: under every window and decision (their overlays start at z 50) and over the page. The play screen is padded by the panel's width while it is open, so it sits beside the map. A slim header (*Side panel* and ✕) and five tabs: 💰 Market (💰 Sell and 🛍️ Buy inside), 🎯 Challenges, ✈️ Travel, 🔨 Workshop, 🏦 Investments. Market shows its two halves as a second tab list. | not drawn; no padding, no style element |
| One logic | A tab draws its window's own content: each of the six windows' elements is built exactly as before and handed to `v115Dock.slot`, which gives it back untouched unless the panel is docked; docked, the window's header, body and footer (the very same elements, with the very same handlers) are drawn in the tab panel and the backdrop and the dialog box (`role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, the focus trap) are left out. | the very same element |
| One tab at a time | Every way in sets the window's own flag through `updateUiState`, which hands each update to `v115DockNormalize`: docked, the window just opened stays open and the others close; its tab and Market's half are remembered in the interface state (`v115DockTab`, `v115DockSide`) for the session. A save carries only the theme from the interface state, so it never carries the panel. | the very same state object |
| Tabs follow the buttons | A tab can open exactly when its window's button could: on the player's turn, what the guided first match has unlocked (the same words as the refused key), and Investments while regional investments are on (otherwise dimmed, saying where its switch is). A dimmed tab is focusable, says why (tooltip and `aria-describedby`) and shows the reason when pressed. | — |
| Keys | Docked, the six windows are not modals, so they hold no key back: C T R M W open their tabs (a window's own key still closes it), G toggles the map, Space ends the turn. Behind a real window or decision only Escape acts, as before. ← → Home End move between tabs (automatic activation; ↑ ↓ stay the page's). | V10.1's key rules |
| Focus | A labelled region (`role="region"`, "Side panel") with a tab list, not a dialog: focus moves to the selected tab when the panel opens and when its tab changes from outside, follows the selection from a tab, stays where the player works in the content, never leaves a window or decision open over the panel, and goes back to the opener when the panel closes. Tab moves on out of the panel (no trap). | — |
| Escape, the rescue, decisions | The surface manager tracks the panel as its open side panel, so Escape closes it after any window or decision open over it; while docked, the challenge approach picker (a z-50 popup that V10.1 drew under the Challenges window) opens over the panel and Escape closes it first. Rescue the screen also lowers Investments' flag while docked (V10.1's rescue leaves it up). | V10.1's Escape order and rescue |
| Narrow screens | Narrower than 1024 px (a `matchMedia` listener, only while the switch is on), or outside a match, the six open as windows, as before; an open tab becomes its window when the screen narrows, and the panel again when it widens. The panel's own Find Anything result then opens the window last open. | — |
| Find Anything | While the switch is on the panel is a result of its own ("Side panel", 871 → 872 entries); "side panel", "docked panel" and "sidebar" put it first, and while the switch is off its switch. The six windows' results open their tabs through their own routes. | the very same index |
| Release check | A row that could not run (`skipped: true`) is reported as skipped by the release check too: the suite's detail says "(N skipped)", the row is neither a pass nor a failure, and a suite where nothing ran does not pass. (V10.1's rendered-markup rows keep their raw pass with a SKIPPED: note, as the LAB shows them.) | no suite reports a skipped row in the runners |
| Self-tests | Suite `v115dp` (40 checks): the switch's default, scope and registration; off and narrow change nothing (the same objects, the very same element); the five tabs over the six windows, which keep their ids; the panel's id and its row in the table; a side panel in the manager, nothing blocking; one tab at a time; every opener, Find Anything's results and the five cards that open one of the six; one way through `updateUiState` (read from the source); the keys; R and M; the guided first match; Investments and the turn; the region, the tab lists, arrow keys, focus and no trap; the same content and handlers; Escape, the approach picker and the rescue; decisions and windows on top; nothing blocked; narrow screens; the session memory and no save; Find Anything's result; the switch's row; the map walk; labels; no clock and no random draw; rules stamp 1010 and old saves move for move; the rendered markup; Team Mode; the release check's skipped rows. | — |

**Every way in, docked** (each was a window in V10.1 and still is with the switch off or on a narrow screen):

| Way in | Opens |
|---|---|
| Action bar and full action bar: 🎯 Challenges, ✈️ Travel, 💰 Market, 🛍️ Resource Market, 🔨 Workshop, 🏦 Investments | their tabs (Market › Sell, Market › Buy) |
| Quick bar: 🎯 Challenges, 🗺️ Explore, 💰 Sell Resources, 🛍️ Resource Market, 🏦 Investments | their tabs |
| Keys C, T, R, M, W | Challenges, Travel, Market › Sell, Market › Buy, Workshop |
| Find Anything: the six windows' results; "Side panel" | their tabs; the tab last open |
| What's in the game: Challenge approaches, More challenges, Procedural Challenges, Bigger wagers (Open Challenges); Regional investments (Open Investments) | Challenges; Investments |
| 🧠 INTELLIGENCE, the win-race chip, Why?, Ask answers and Co-Pilot "Open …" buttons (`openIntentNav`) | the tab they name |
| The guided first match: its buttons for what it has unlocked | their tabs; the rest wait, dimmed |
| The windows' own links: the Sell Market's Workshop buttons, the Workshop's Open Market, the map's region preview Travel | Workshop; Market › Buy; Travel |
| Ask deep links, the character abilities (Market Insight, Scout Ahead) | Market › Buy, Workshop, Travel; Market › Sell, Travel |

**Where the brief, the map and the file disagreed (and what UX-5 did).**

- *"The 12 blocking decisions"*: the 12 are V10.1's ids the map marks *decision*; UX-2 registered 5 more (Co-Pilot proposal, Leave match, confirmations, load preview, diplomatic breach). All 17 open over the panel (`v115dp_27` walks them with the 6 action windows, Loans and the 24 tool windows, which stay as they were). Five of the 12 never open in V10.1 (§11.1).
- *Five tabs, one of them off by default*: Investments is a tab of its own, dimmed while regional investments are off, saying where to turn them on. It never opens the Investments window while they are off.
- *Co-Pilot "Do It"*: a card's Do It carries out its action by hand (travel, a sale, a purchase) and opens no window; its navigation buttons ("Open Market" and the like) and the Intelligence and Ask buttons do, through `openIntentNav`, and those open the tabs. *The guided first match* opens no window itself: it decides which buttons (and so which tabs) it shows.
- *The approach picker*: in V10.1 it opens under the Challenges window (z 50 under z 500) and Escape closes the window instead. Docked it opens over the panel and Escape closes it first; as windows, V10.1's order is kept.
- *The rescue*: V10.1's rescue lowers five of the six flags but not Investments'; docked, the rescue lowers all six, so it always closes the panel. Off, the rescue is V10.1's.
- *The release check and skipped rows*: it counted a skipped row (`passed: false, skipped: true`) as a failure, so a check that cannot run in the LAB (no static renderer) would have failed the suite; it now reports it as skipped.

**Deferred (not this package):** the six action windows (Negotiations, Contracts, Infrastructure, Expeditions, Shop, Sabotage) and Loans as extra tabs, and the 24 tool windows (Q3: a later round). The optional floating widgets (off by default) keep their own positions and may sit over the panel's lower corner; the classic layout's floating quick bar is centred on the window, not on the page beside the panel. The remembered tab lasts while the page is open (not across a reload). With the switch off, Escape with the approach picker open keeps V10.1's order. UX-6 turns the panel on by default and adds the classic switch. *UX-6: the floating widgets, the classic quick bar and turn pill make room for the panel, the panel is on by default and the classic switch is added (§20); the six action windows, Loans and the 24 tool windows still wait for a later round.*

## 20. UX-6: Switch-over — what changed

All new code is in SECTION 20BT (one look for the new screens), SECTION 20BU (the switch-over: defaults, the one move and notice, the classic switch, the twins, What's New), SECTION 20BV (the clean-ups carried from UX-3 to UX-5) and SECTION 20BW (a real V10.1 save, the map walk in both layouts and suite `v116so`), between SECTION 20BS and SECTION 21. Every in-place edit elsewhere is marked `// V11.0 (SECTION 20BT)` to `(SECTION 20BW)`: 178 marked lines (159 places, none unmarked).

| Area | What changed | Classic layout on |
|---|---|---|
| Defaults | `findAnythingEnabled`, `settingsLayersEnabled`, `whatsInTheGameEnabled`, `dockedPanelEnabled` default to on (DEFAULT_GAME_SETTINGS and the Co-Pilot catalog; the four sections' chip says *On by default*). No other default changed: V10.1's 599 defaults keep their fingerprint. | all four read as off |
| The one move | `v116BootSwitchOver` runs once per session on the settings the profile store produced. No record and a returning player (a first-run record or stored preferences): the four switches are set on, the record says *moved*, and the main menu shows the notice. No record and a new player: nothing moves, the record says *new*. A record: nothing moves again. The notice is marked seen when the main menu draws it. Blocked or failing storage never throws (a blocked profile starts new, with no notice). | — |
| The notice | A labelled region at the top of the main menu: what the new layout is, and "Prefer how V10.1 looked? Turn on 🕰️ Classic layout (V10.1)" with where the switch is. *Keep the new layout* (or Escape inside it) closes it; *Use the classic layout* turns the classic switch on. | — |
| The classic switch | `classicLayoutV101` (boolean, false, profile, remembered between visits, never in a save; Co-Pilot catalog; settings search). Read inside the four predicates (`v110FindAnythingOn`, `v113LayersOn`, `v114PageOn`, `v115DockOn`), so every V11.0 screen goes off at once: no Find Anything (Ctrl/⌘+K is V10.1's again), the Simple view and the 11 tabs, no What's in the game, no side panel, V10.1's surface table. The four switches' values are never touched. Turning it on or off closes the V11.0 windows that were open and says where the switch is. | — |
| Where the classic switch is | The main menu (in both layouts: the way back), ⭐ Essentials (after the 12 choices; its home in the layered view), Find Anything ("classic", "classic layout", "old layout", "v10.1" put it first; a layered result opens Essentials at its row), and the classic view's Settings › Interface & Accessibility › 🕰️ Classic layout, which lists the four switches it holds off with the player's own choice for each. | the main menu and Settings › Interface & Accessibility |
| The twins (item 4, the twins only) | With the classic layout off, the Team Brain toggle and slider read the label table (the slider: *Travel Discipline Strength*) and the Floating UI row and its options object's change label read it too (*Floating UI Conflict Control Options*). | V10.1's titles |
| One look | SECTION 20BT's shared styles on Find Anything, ⭐ Essentials and the group bar (and the layered rows and blocks), What's in the game and the side panel: one type scale (rem sizes and the 11 px the Text size setting scales; the panel's 10.5 px tab labels and the 10 px chips and notes are 11 px now), one button shape in three sizes and an icon button, one spacing for heads, bands, cards and options, and one focus ring (keyboard focus only, sky-600, 2 px out). The selected result and the card Find Anything opened share the same mark. White text on green is green-700 and the counts on coloured buttons are drawn at full strength (4.5:1 or better in every theme); the side panel's card is drawn solid (Retro's card is 90% opaque and the panel has no dimmed backdrop behind it), so the old windows' text reads in the panel as it does in its own window. | not drawn |
| a. Negotiations | In the layered view the moved "more negotiation options" block leaves out the three options the section draws itself (auto-accept, counter-offer suggestions, confirm expensive proposals): six options, each once, all writing the same `negotiationOptions` fields as before. | V10.1's two lists |
| b. Room for the panel | While the panel is open, its style element also moves the Ask and Guardian buttons, the Widget Dock and the notification lane to its left and centres the classic layout's floating quick bar and turn pill on the page beside it. | Build | Settings | Find Anything | What's in the game |
|---|---|---|---|
| V10.1 build | 31.4 ms (dev 607) | — | — |
| Before UX-6, its default (the four off) | 20.6 ms (dev 620) | — | — |
| Before UX-6, the four on | 32.4 ms (dev 1,262) | 24.8 ms (dev 582) | 63.7 ms (dev 710) |
| After UX-6, the default (new layout) | 37.4 ms (dev 1,252) | 25.7 ms (dev 596) | 49.2 ms (dev 687) |
| After UX-6, classic layout on | 27.3 ms (dev 653) | — | — |

**Where the brief, the map and the file disagreed (and what UX-6 did).**

- The map's §1 said UX-6 "restyles everything"; the brief says the new V11.0 screens only and the classic layout untouched. UX-6 followed the brief: the shared styles are on Find Anything, ⭐ Essentials and the group bar, What's in the game and the side panel, nowhere else.
- Item 4 (the shared label table for rows drawn from old sections) was asked as "Twins only" and done that way: the Team Brain toggle and slider, and the Floating UI row and its options label. Floating UI Conflict Control is drawn once in the layered view, so only its options object needed a name of its own.
- The file held no real V10.1 save (only fixtures). One was made with the V10.1 build in the browser (a Day 4 Team Mode match), shrunk, and embedded in SECTION 20BW with the V10.1 build's own three-day results as the reference.
- What's New already had a V11.0 entry from UX-2 (V110_CHANGELOG). UX-6's entry covers the whole round and is the one drawn; V110_CHANGELOG stays in the file unchanged.
- Classic Settings is V10.1 apart from the new switch rows, as the brief asks, and also the counts those rows move (sections 86 → 91, settings 599 → 604) and "6 settings differ from the defaults" while the classic switch is on.

**Still open:** A setting whose row draws only when its parent switch is on (or inside a closed group) still lands on its section, or on the Teams gate, not the row. A widget the player dragged, or Floating UI Conflict Control relocated, keeps its place when the panel opens. V10.1's own Guardian and Ask buttons overlap each other by 9 px (unchanged). Layered Settings draws about twice the elements of the classic Simple view, so it opens a few milliseconds slower (see the times). The in-game LAB runner's numbers were not re-measured (the node runner was).
